People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, Dr. Smith. Thank you so much for seeing me today. 

Um... well, I guess what finally pushed me to come in is... my eating has been just a little all over the place lately. I mean, it's probably not a huge deal, but... some weeks I barely touch my dinner. And then... well, it's a little embarrassing, honestly, but other nights I find myself just standing in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone’s gone to bed. I know it's a terrible habit. 

Is... is that the sort of thing you need to know? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

agitation.\n  manifestation: At my busiest I sign up for committees, message people about workshops, start several blog drafts, and make big plans for the garden all in the same day.\n- key: Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, I know I'm not in my twenties anymore, and I'm sure I could stand to be a little healthier, but... I don't really obsess over my weight or my shape. I really try to just be grateful for the body I have, you know?

I don't know, the whole house just feels kind of gray and heavy? \n\nLike, I'll just be sitting there at the kitchen table feeling sort of empty inside, even though absolutely nothing terrible has happened that day. It's silly, really. I always try to just snap myself out of it so I'm not a bother to anyone. \n\nIs... is that what you mean by feeling down? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of examples.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these 'gray and heavy' feelings, and how often do they happen—would you say most days for the past two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's a little hard to say exactly, because I really do try to just push through it and keep a smile on my face. But... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I should be... those gray periods, where I just feel so guilty for no reason and can't seem to care about my usual hobbies... \n\nIf I add them all up, it's probably been going on for... maybe about seven months? I know that sounds like a long time, and I really should have done a better job of pulling myself together sooner. I'm sorry if that's not what you were expecting to hear. Does... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Over these past seven months, would you say you've felt this 'gray and heavy' most days, or does it come and go with better days in between?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... it definitely comes and goes. I really wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around moping every single day, because I do have good days! \n\nIt's just... like I was saying before, about how I'll just sit at the kitchen table feeling so empty? I try really hard to focus on those better days so I'm not bringing anyone down. \n\nIs... is that the kind of detail you need? That's actually... well, that's sort of what finally pushed me to come in and see you. I really didn't want to make a fuss, but my eating has just been so all over the place lately. \n\nLike I said, some weeks I'll just sit there and barely touch my dinner, which I feel terrible about because my husband cooks and I don't want to seem ungrateful. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, yes, I definitely have some really good stretches! I'd say there have been times, maybe several weeks at a time, where my mood was just... fine. You know? Not too high, not too low, just perfectly even. I try really hard to maintain that balance.

But, um... even when I'm feeling totally okay in my mood, I guess there are still a few... little things. Nothing major, I promise! It's just... well, even during those good weeks, I still felt pretty certain that some people online were keeping track of the garden photos I post. Just keeping a close eye on them. And... sometimes I'd still hear just a quick, brief remark when I was by myself. Not a full conversation or anything scary, just a little comment here and there. 

I hope that makes sense? I really don't want it to sound like I'm doing worse than I am, because I really do feel quite stable during those weeks. Is that the kind of detail you needed?

I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly, and I hope I'm not making it sound more dramatic than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do these ups and downs in your eating—skipping dinner some nights, snacking after everyone's asleep other nights—tend to happen mainly during those 'gray and heavy' stretches, or do they happen just as often on your better days too?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's mostly during those down times, I think. That's actually... well, that's sort of what finally pushed me to come in and see you. I really didn't want to make a fuss, but my eating has just been so all over the place lately. \n\nLike I said, some weeks I'll just sit there and barely touch my dinner, which I feel terrible about because my husband cooks and I don't want to seem ungrateful. But then... and this is a little embarrassing to admit... other nights I'll just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. I know it's a terrible habit, and I really should have better self-control. \n\nIt just seems to happen more when I'm in one of those heavy stretches. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the whole picture, even the parts I'm not very proud of.\n\nDoctor: Aside from how much you're eating, do you find yourself worrying a lot about your weight or body shape, or feeling upset about how you look?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I don't know, the whole house just feels kind of gray and heavy? \n\nLike, I'll just be sitting there at the kitchen table feeling sort of empty inside, even though absolutely nothing terrible has happened that day. It's silly, really. I always try to just snap myself out of it so I'm not a bother to anyone. \n\nIs... is that what you mean by feeling down? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of examples.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these 'gray and heavy' feelings, and how often do they happen—would you say most days for the past two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's a little hard to say exactly, because I really do try to just push through it and keep a smile on my face. I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? But... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I should be... those gray periods, where I just feel so guilty for no reason and can't seem to care about my usual hobbies... \n\nIf I add them all up, it's probably been going on for... maybe about seven months? I know that sounds like a long time, and I really should have done a better job of pulling myself together sooner. I'm sorry if that's not what you were expecting to hear. Does... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Over these past seven months, would you say you've felt this 'gray and heavy' most days, or does it come and go with better days in between?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... it definitely comes and goes. I really wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around moping every single day, because I do have good days! \n\nIt's just... like I was saying before, about how I'll just sit at the kitchen table feeling so empty? It's more like I go through these stretches where everything just feels that way, you know, gray and heavy, even when absolutely nothing bad has happened. But then the stretch will pass and I'll have days where I can push past it and be useful around the house again. I try really hard to focus on those better days so I'm not bringing anyone down. \n\nIs... is that the kind of detail you need? I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly, and I hope I'm not making it sound more dramatic than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do these ups and downs in your eating—skipping dinner some nights, snacking after everyone's asleep other nights—tend to happen mainly during those 'gray and heavy' stretches, or do they happen just as often on your better days too?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's mostly during those down times, I think. That's actually... well, that's sort of what finally pushed me to come in and see you. I really didn't want to make a fuss, but my eating has just been so all over the place lately. \n\nLike I said, some weeks I'll just sit there and barely touch my dinner, which I feel terrible about because my husband cooks and I don't want to seem ungrateful. But then... and this is a little embarrassing to admit... other nights I'll just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. I know it's a terrible habit, and I really should have better self-control. \n\nIt just seems to happen more when I'm in one of those heavy stretches. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the whole picture, even the parts I'm not very proud of.\n\nDoctor: Aside from how much you're eating, do you find yourself worrying a lot about your weight or body shape, or feeling upset about how you look?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, I know I'm not in my twenties anymore, and I'm sure I could stand to be a little healthier, but... I don't really obsess over my weight or my shape. I really try to just be grateful for the body I have, you know? \n\nIt's really just the... the guilt about the snacking itself and feeling out of control that bothers me, not so much how I look in the mirror. I hope that's okay? I just don't want you to think I'm overly vain or anything. Does that help clear things up?\n\nDoctor: Before these past seven months, had you ever gone through similar 'gray and heavy' stretches in the past, or is this the first time in your life you've noticed feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I've always tried to be a fairly positive person, you know? So I really don't think I've struggled with it before now. It's really just been recently. \n\nIf I... if I really think about it and add up all those gray periods—the times where I just feel so guilty and kind of lose interest in my normal routine—it all adds up to about seven months total. I definitely haven't been dealing with it my whole life or anything like that, I promise. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that clearly enough? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: During these gray, heavy stretches, have you noticed any changes in your sleep, your energy levels, or your ability to concentrate on things like conversations or tasks around the house?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, I try to keep up with everything. I really do. I make sure the house is tidy and my husband has what he needs. But... I guess during those heavy times, I do feel a little more tired than usual. Not completely exhausted or anything! Just... maybe a bit slower to get going in the mornings. \n\nAnd as for sleep... I sleep okay, mostly. I mean, sometimes it takes me a little while to wind down, but I don't want to complain. I think my concentration is fine, really. I might lose my train of thought here and there, but nothing too terrible, I promise. I really try my best to stay focused. \n\nI hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: During these gray, heavy stretches, would you say you're still able to keep up with your usual responsibilities and social activities without much difficulty, or do you find it considerably harder to manage things like household tasks or seeing friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really do try my best to keep everything running smoothly, I promise. I don't want to let anyone down. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, it has been a little bit harder to manage things during those times.\n\nI've, um... I've fallen a little behind on my facilitator training, and some private blog work I do. It’s just that my projects and... well, my explanations started feeling a bit hard to organize. I'm sure I just need to focus a little more to stay on top of it, though! \n\nAnd... well, I feel terrible even saying this, but there’s been a little bit of tension at home. Just a little! But it has made it slightly harder to keep up with our normal routines, my husband and I. I really am trying my best to get back on track with everything. I hope it's okay that I shared that? I just don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or not doing my part. Does that answer your question all right?\n\nDoctor: During the 'better days' within these past seven months, do you feel completely back to your normal self with no lingering low mood at all, and have there been any stretches of several weeks or more where you felt entirely fine before the gray periods came back?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I definitely have some really good stretches! I'd say there have been times, maybe several weeks at a time, where my mood was just... fine. You know? Not too high, not too low, just perfectly even. I try really hard to maintain that balance.\n\nBut, um... even when I'm feeling totally okay in my mood, I guess there are still a few... little things. Nothing major, I promise! It's just... well, even during those good weeks, I still felt pretty certain that some people online were keeping track of the garden photos I post. Just keeping a close eye on them. And... sometimes I'd still hear just a quick, brief remark when I was by myself. Not a full conversation or anything scary, just a little comment here and there. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I really don't want it to sound like I'm doing worse than I am, because I really do feel quite stable during those weeks. Is that the kind of detail you needed?\n\nDoctor: Thinking back over the entire seven months, about how long have you been noticing these experiences—like sensing people are watching your garden photos or hearing brief comments when you're alone—and have they been happening continuously, even during the weeks when your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's actually been going on a little longer than the seven months, if I'm being completely honest. I hope it's okay to admit that. I just... I didn't want to make a big fuss about it before. \n\nI'd say the... the feeling about the photos, and hearing those little brief comments... it's been happening for about a year and a half now. I know it's a terrible habit, and I really should have better self-control. \n\nIt just seems to happen more when I'm in one of those heavy stretches. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the whole picture, even the parts I'm not very proud of.\n\nDoctor: Aside from how much you're eating, do you find yourself worrying a lot about your weight or body shape, or feeling upset about how you look?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, I know I'm not in my twenties anymore, and I'm sure I could stand to be a little healthier, but... I don't really obsess over my weight or my shape. I really try to just be grateful for the body I have, you know? \n\nIt's really just the... the guilt about the snacking itself and feeling out of control that bothers me, not so much how I look in the mirror. I hope that's okay? I just don't want you to think I'm overly vain or anything. It's really just been recently. \n\nIf I... if I really think about it and add up all those gray periods—the times where I just feel so guilty and kind of lose interest in my normal routine—it all adds up to about seven months total. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: During these gray, heavy stretches, have you noticed any changes in your sleep, your energy levels, or your ability to concentrate on things like conversations or tasks around the house?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, I try to keep up with everything. I really do. I make sure the house is tidy and my husband has what he needs. But... I guess during those heavy times, I do feel a little more tired than usual. Not completely exhausted or anything! Just... maybe a bit slower to get going in the mornings. \n\nAnd as for sleep... I sleep okay, mostly. I mean, sometimes it takes me a little while to wind down, but I don't want to complain. I think my concentration is fine, really. I might lose my train of thought here and there, but nothing too terrible, I promise. I really try my best to stay focused. \n\nI hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: During these gray, heavy stretches, would you say you're still able to keep up with your usual responsibilities and social activities without much difficulty, or do you find it considerably harder to manage things like household tasks or seeing friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really do try my best to keep everything running smoothly, I promise. I don't want to let anyone down. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, it has been a little bit harder to manage things during those times.\n\nI've, um... But it has made it slightly harder to keep up with our normal routines, my husband and I. I really am trying my best to get back on track with everything. I hope it's okay that I shared that? I just don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or not doing my part. Does that answer your question all right?\n\nDoctor: During the 'better days' within these past seven months, do you feel completely back to your normal self with no lingering low mood at all, and have there been any stretches of several weeks or more where you felt entirely fine before the gray periods came back?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I definitely have some really good stretches! I'd say there have been times, maybe several weeks at a time, where my mood was just... fine. You know? Not too high, not too low, just perfectly even. I try really hard to maintain that balance.\n\nBut, um... even when I'm feeling totally okay in my mood, I guess there are still a few... little things. Nothing major, I promise!
